--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 1:**
- The champion is surprisingly simple: 1-state Repulsion with high gain (4.75). This spreads robots out but doesn't direct them to the upper (BLUE) strip.
- Score 15606 is quite high (out of 1200×16=19200 max), meaning robots spend a lot of time outside.
- The mission requires moving toward BLUE walls and staying in the upper strip. Pure repulsion spreads robots randomly.
- We need FSMs that: (1) move toward BLUE (GoToColor with clr=3), (2) detect black floor to stay put, (3) avoid GREEN walls.

**Key insight:** GoToColor with clr=3 (blue) should drive robots to the upper strip. Once there (black floor detected), Stop or low-exploration behavior keeps them there.

**Strategy:**
1. **Survival**: Copy champion exactly.
2. **Exploitation**: Tweak the repulsion gain slightly; add 2-state variants with repulsion + GoToColor.
3. **Exploration**: 2-3 state machines using GoToColor(blue)/GoAwayColor(green) + Stop on black floor detection.

**Color mapping note:** Colors 1-6: I'll assume 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan. Blue walls = clr=3, Green walls = clr=2.

**FSM designs:**
1. Champion copy (repulsion 4.75)
2. 2-state: GoToColor(blue) → on black floor → Stop
3. 2-state: GoToColor(blue) → on black floor (high prob) → Stop; Stop → on gray → GoToColor
4. 3-state: Explore → see blue → GoToColor(blue) → black floor → Stop
5. 2-state: GoAwayColor(green) → black floor → Stop
6. 2-state: GoToColor(blue) with transition to Stop on black
7. Repulsion variant (gain 4.0)
8. 3-state with GoToColor blue, black detection, stop
9. 2-state zigzag + GoToColor blue
10. Mixed: exploration + GoToColor blue on color perception
11. 2-state GoToColor blue + stop with probability transitions
12. GoAwayColor green + black floor stop
13. 3-state: GoToColor, Stop, Exploration
14. Repulsion + GoToColor combo

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 2:**
The champion (score 5140.76) is a 3-state FSM:
- State 0: GoToColor(blue=3) → if black floor (p=0.9) → Stop; if sees green (p=0.7) → Stop
- State 1: Stop → if gray floor (p=0.8) → Stop(itself? No, destination 1 from state 1... wait)
  - Actually: n1x0 destination=1 means "the other state not state 1" = with 3 states, leaving state 1, others are [0,2], so D=1 → state 2
  - n1x1 destination=0 → state 0
- State 2: Exploration(rwm=20) → if sees blue (p=0.8) → state 0 (GoToColor blue)

So the logic is: GoToColor(blue) → black floor → Stop; Stop → gray → Exploration; Exploration → sees blue → GoToColor(blue).

This is excellent! The robot moves toward blue, stops when on black (upper strip), and if it drifts to gray, starts exploring again, and re-locks onto blue.

**Key fix:** The stop condition `n1x0` with destination 1 (→ state 2 Exploration) fires on gray, which is correct - if we're on gray we're not in the target, so explore. The small random probability (0.02) occasionally breaks out too.

**Exploitation ideas:**
1. Keep champion exactly
2. Adjust black floor probability (0.9 → 0.95, 0.85)
3. Adjust gray floor probability in Stop state (0.8 → 0.9, 0.7)
4. Add more transitions to champion states
5. Try GoAwayColor(green) instead of/in addition to GoToColor(blue)
6. Adjust exploration rate (rwm=20 → 10, 30)
7. Try 4-state variants with GoAwayColor + GoToColor

**Note on transition indexing fix:** When state 1 has 2 transitions (n1=2), n1x0 and n1x1 both need destination, condition, and params. Destination D is indexed among OTHER states: with 3 states, leaving state 1, others are [0,2], so D=0→state0, D=1→state2.

## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 6:**
The champion (2634.17) held its position - none of the round 6 machines beat it. The best performers this round were all variants of the champion topology but slightly worse. We seem to be in a tight local optimum around:
- p0x0=0.85, p0x1=0.8, p1x0=0.8, p1x1=0.05, p2x0=0.85, p2x1=0.6

**What didn't work:** Adding extra transitions (3rd transition to state1, 3rd transition to state0), changing p1x1 to 0.04, changing p2x0 to 0.88.

**What's promising:** The champion parameters seem quite stable. The scores are converging around 2800-2900 for close variants.

**Strategy for Round 7:**
- Keep champion
- Fine-tune around champion: try small perturbations of each parameter independently
- Try very slightly different p values: p0x0=0.83, 0.84, 0.86; p1x0=0.78, 0.82; p1x1=0.05 seems optimal; p2x0=0.83, 0.84, 0.86; p2x1=0.58, 0.62
- Try exploration: different topology like having state1 transition to GoAwayGreen on seeing green walls
- Try combining: different LED colors to see if they matter
- Try genuinely different structure: 2-state with combined cues

**Destination indexing (3-state):**
- Leaving state 0: others=[1,2], D=0→state1, D=1→state2
- Leaving state 1: others=[0,2], D=0→state0, D=1→state2
- Leaving state 2: others=[0,1], D=0→state0, D=1→state1

- 4-state machines are worse

**The champion is very robust.** We need to either:

## Reasoning

**Analysis of Round 8:**
Champion still holds at 2634.17. We're deeply stuck in the local optimum. The champion parameters are:
- p0x0=0.85 (black→Stop in GoAwayGreen), p0x1=0.8 (sees blue→GoToBlue)
- p1x0=0.8 (gray→GoToBlue in Stop), p1x1=0.05 (random→GoAwayGreen)
- p2x0=0.85 (black→Stop in GoToBlue), p2x1=0.6 (sees green→GoAwayGreen)

**What we've learned:**
- p1x0=0.76-0.78 gives ~2788-2812 (close but worse)
- p2x0=0.88, p2x1=0.55 gives 2815 (close)
- p2x1=0.56 gives 2816 (close)
- Changing escape from random to color-perception in state1 hurts badly
- 3-state topology with GoAwayGreen/Stop/GoToBlue is optimal

**New ideas for round 9 (last exploitation rounds):**
1. Cross-combine: p1x0=0.78 with p2x1=0.56 (both individually near-optimal)
2. Try p1x0=0.77, 0.79 - narrowing in on that parameter
3. Try p2x1=0.57, 0.59 - narrowing in
4. Try p2x0=0.86, 0.87 combined with p2x1=0.56
5. Try p0x0=0.85 with p0x1=0.77, 0.79 (haven't tried these)
6. Try p1x1=0.045, 0.055 - haven't tried these exact values
7. Genuine exploration: completely different structure to escape local optimum

**Error fix:** I must ensure `--n1x1 D` always has D right after it. The repeated error suggests I'm writing `--n1x1` without the destination. Let me be extremely careful: `--n1x1 0` means "transition 1 of state 1, destination index 0 (=state0 when leaving state1 with 3 states)".

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
OBJECTIVE. At every simulation step the loop function adds +1 to the score for each robot that is NOT inside the target region. The score is MINIMISED, so a lower score means the swarm reached the region earlier and more robots stayed in it.

THE TARGET REGION is the upper strip of the arena. It is NOT identified by a unique floor colour: the floor is BLACK in BOTH the upper and the lower strip, and GRAY in between. Only the UPPER black strip is rewarded. Sitting on the lower black strip scores exactly as badly as standing in the middle of the arena.

HOW TO TELL THE TWO STRIPS APART. At the start of the run the arena walls light up: the two walls bordering the REWARDED upper strip are BLUE, the two walls bordering the penalised lower strip are GREEN. Colour is the only reliable cue.

CONSEQUENCE FOR THE FSM. A black-floor condition is useful to detect that a strip has been reached, but on its own it sends half the swarm to the wrong strip. It must be combined with colour-driven motion - move toward BLUE, or move away from GREEN - to reach the correct one. Once inside, robots should stay rather than keep exploring, since every step spent outside costs a point. Clustering tightly is NOT rewarded: only being inside counts.

# 2. THE ARENA FOR THIS RUN
The arena is a regular polygon with 6 walls, inner radius 0.77 m. 16 e-puck robots start scattered inside a disc of radius 0.56 m around the centre, with random orientations. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.52 m: neighbour-count conditions only see robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).
For reference, the published setup uses 20 robots, an inner radius of 0.87 m and a range of 0.50 m, so thresholds tuned for that setup may need adjusting here.

# 3. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 4. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type. A state that reacts to several different situations is often stronger than a state with one transition, and random sampling produces that variety by default.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 5. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every state that exists must declare `--n<I>`, and every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.
4. Never point a transition at its own state.

# 6. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
